is a POVM. From our implied notation, we have
K=q^{\kappa},M=q^{r}
and
B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}q^{\beta}
.

Key Steps outlining the proof and the proxy ‘state’
: We follow an analogous sequence of steps as employed in proof of Thm.
2
with two differences as stated below. Firstly, the proxy ‘state’ that we define here will not be guaranteed to have unit trace. Secondly, the binning rate and the change-of-measure factor is non-trivial. The additional term will enable us handle the error in the possible
\underline{b}-
index that is not communicated to the decoder. We begin by identifying the proxy ‘state’. For
a\in[K]
, let

\displaystyle T_{a}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{S_{a}}{MB}\mbox{ have SCD }T_{a}=\sum_{t=1}^{d^{n}}\nu_{ta}\outerproduct{x_{ta}}{x_{ta}},\mbox{ i.e., }\innerproduct{x_{ta}}{x_{\hatt a}}=\delta_{\hat{t}t}\mbox{ for }a\in[K]\mbox{ and }\sigma_{K}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{a\in[K]}T_{a}\otimes\outerproduct{a}{a},

(146)

\displaystyle\alpha\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\boldsymbol{{\mathfrak{E}_{\mbox{\tiny sim}}}}(\outerproduct{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}})\mbox{ where }\ket{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{t=1}^{d^{n}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\frac{1}{K}\sqrt{\nu_{ta}}\ket{\olinex_{ta}~a}\otimes\ket{x_{ta}~a}\mbox{ is the canonical purification of }\sigma_{K}.

(147)

Next we define the CPTP maps that yield the intermediate operator
\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}
. We define the POVM
\Theta\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\tilde{\theta}_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\theta_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\otimes\outerproduct{\ulinek}{\ulinek}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{H}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{W}^{\kappa}}):(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in\mathcal{W}^{\kappa}\times\mathcal{W}^{r}\times\mathcal{W}^{b},\theta_{\underline{k},0}\}
with
\theta_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}
defined as in (
141
). We define

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\overline{\Delta}_{\mathcal{Y}^{n}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\overline{\Delta}_{y^{n}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in\mathcal{W}^{\kappa}\times\mathcal{W}^{r}\times\mathcal{W}^{\beta}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct{\ulk~ \ulm~\ulb}{\ulk~ \ulm~\ulb}p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|c(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})):y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathcal{H}_{K}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{M}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B},\mathcal{Y}^{n}).

(148)

Let

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\boldsymbol{{\mathfrak{E}_{\mbox{\tiny int}}}}(\outerproduct{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}),~\mbox{ where }\boldsymbol{{\mathfrak{E}_{\mbox{\tiny int}}}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(i_{X}^{n}\!\otimes\!\tr_{K}\!\otimes i_{\mathcal{Y}^{n}})\circ(i_{X}^{n}\!\otimes\!i_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\!\otimes\!\mathscr{E}^{\overline{\Delta}}\!)\!\circ\!(i_{X}^{n}\!\otimes\!i_{\mathcal{H}_{K}}\!\otimes\!\mathscr{E}^{\Theta})\!\!\!

(149)

and as defined before
\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny S}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\boldsymbol{{\mathfrak{E}_{\mbox{\tiny sim}}}}(\outerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{\ulineK}^{\otimes n}}\!\!}{\!\phi_{\rho_{\ulineK}^{\otimes n}}\!})
where
\boldsymbol{{\mathfrak{E}_{\mbox{\tiny sim}}}}(\cdot)
is as defined in (
144
). Our proof will derive upper bounds on each of the three terms in the right hand side of

\displaystyle||\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}}-\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny S}}||_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}}-\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}||_{1}+||\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}-\alpha||_{1}+||\alpha-\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny S}}||_{1}

(150)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}}-\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}||_{1}+||\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}-\alpha||_{1}+||\outerproduct{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}-\outerproduct{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}||_{1}.

Let us now characterize
\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}},\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}},\alpha
and
\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny S}}
. To obtain this, we recall the POVMs
\theta,\Theta
and the original POVM
\lambda_{\mathcal{Y}}
. Our characterization of
\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}},\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}},\alpha
follows a sequence of steps identical to that in proof of Thm.
2
found in Appendix
A-A
. Following an identical sequence of steps as in Appendix
A-A
, it can be verified that

\displaystyle\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny O}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\omega}\lambda_{y^{n}}\sqrt{\omega}}{\tr(\omega\lambda_{y^{n}})}\otimes\outerproduct{y^{n}}{y^{n}},~~\alpha_{\mbox{\tiny I}}=\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}))}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\outerproduct{y^{n}}{y^{n}}

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}))}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\outerproduct{y^{n}}{y^{n}}.

We are now set to analyze each of the terms
\norm{\alphaorig-\alphaint}_{1}
,
\norm{\alphaint-\alpha}_{1}
and
\norm{\ketbra{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}} -\ketbra{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}}_{1}
.

Upper Bound on
\norm{\alphaorig-\alphaint}_{1}
: We now employ random coding. Our generator matrices
G_{c},G_{m},G_{b}
and the random dither vector
D^{n}
are mutually independent and uniformly distributed. The codewords
C(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}):(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in\mathcal{W}^{\kappa}\times\mathcal{W}^{r}\times\mathcal{W}^{\beta}
are uniformly distributed over
\mathcal{F}_{q}^{n}
and pairwise independent. Suppose
\mathbf{P}
denotes this distribution of the random code, then
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\norm{\alphaorig-\alphaint}_{1}\right\}
is an instance of the generalized quantum covering lemma stated and proved in Sec.
C
. From Lemma
9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\norm{\alphaorig-\alphaint}_{1}\right\}\leq\exp\left\{-n\left(\frac{(\kappa+\beta+r)\log q}{n}-I(XY;W)_{\sigma}-\log q+H(p_{W})-5\eta\right)\right\}

(151)

where the mutual information quantity is computed with respect to quantum state
\sigma_{XWY}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(i_{X}\otimes\overline{\mathscr{E}}^{{Y|W}}_{p})\circ(i_{X}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\mu})(\outerproduct{\phi_{\rho}}{\phi_{\rho}})
and
(p_{W}(w)=\tr(\mu_{w}\rho):w\in\mathcal{W})
.

Upper Bound on
\norm{\alphaint-\alpha}_{1}
: The bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}}\{\norm{\alphaint-\alpha}_{1}\}
is obtained via the binning analysis. We provide the steps below. Note that

\displaystyle\norm{\alphaint-\alpha}_{1}=\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left( p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) - p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)\mathds{1}_{\{ w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb) \in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left( p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) - p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle+\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)\mathds{1}_{\{ w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb) \notin T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left( p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) - p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)\mathds{1}_{\{ w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb) \in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left( p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) - p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\notin T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}\tr({\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}})\left[p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}))+p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}))\right]\leq\mathtt{t}_{1}+\mathtt{t}_{2}\mbox{ where}

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)\mathds{1}_{\{ w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb) \in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left(\begin{array}{c} p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) ~~~~\\~~~~- p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\end{array}\right)}_{1}

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\frac{2\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\notin T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}\tr({\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}})

(155)

We now analyze
\mathtt{t}_{1}
. Let us define
J(\underline{k},\underline{m})=\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})=1\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})\neq 1,\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}=\uline{0}\}}
. By the encoding rule, we have
J(\underline{k},\underline{m})=1
for all
\underline{k},\underline{m}
. With this, we have

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{1}=\norm{ \sum_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{J\upsilon(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb)\mathds{1}_{\{ w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb) \in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}{\sqrt{\omega}\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}\sqrt{\omega}}\otimes\ketbra{y^{n}} \left( p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulinebstarkm)) - p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb))\right)}_{1}.

We now claim

\displaystyle J\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}\left(p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}))-p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}))\right)~~~~~~~~~~~~

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~=\left(p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\uline{0}))-p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}))\right)\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1\}}\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}

(156)

An argument for (
156
) follows. We consider the different cases for
\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})
. Suppose
\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})=0
, then clearly both LHS and RHS are
0
. Indeed, when
\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})=0
, we have
\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}=0
and
\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1\}}=0
. Suppose
\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})=1
, then by the encoding rule, we have
\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}=b
and both RHS and LHS are zero. Suppose,
\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1
, then by the encoding rule,
\underline{b}^{*}_{\underline{k},\underline{m}}=\uline{0}
and this implies RHS and LHS of (
156
) are identical. We now use this chain of inequalities in analyzing
\mathtt{t}_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathtt{t}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\sum_{y^{n}}\frac{\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1,w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}\tr({\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}})\left[p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\uline{0}))+p_{Y|W}^{n}(y^{n}|w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}))\right]

(157)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\frac{2\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1,w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB\tr(\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb})}\tr({\omega\mu_{\ulinek,\ulinem,\ulineb}}).

Collating the bounds in (
155
) and (
157
), we have

\displaystyle\norm{\alphaint-\alpha}_{1}\leq\sum_{\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b}}\left[\frac{2\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{\ \mathrm{l}(\underline{k},\underline{m})>1,w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\in T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB}+\frac{2\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\mathds{1}_{\{w^{n}(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})\notin T_{\eta}^{n}(p_{W})\}}}{KMB}\right].

(158)

We are now set to compute upper bounds on
\mathbb{E}\{\mathtt{T}_{1}\}+\mathbb{E}\{\mathtt{T}_{2}\}
, where
\mathtt{T}_{1}
and
\mathtt{T}_{2}
are the corresponding random quantities corresponding to a random code wherein
G_{c},G_{m},G_{b}
and the random dither vector
D^{n}
are mutually independent and uniformly distributed. Observe that the above sequence of steps have transformed the analysis to a classical information theory error event analysis. The presence of the measure transformation factor
\upsilon(\underline{k},\underline{m},\underline{b})
implies that the above analysis is identical to a conventional binning analysis that is standard in information theory. We therefore refer the reader to
[
26
, Proof of Thm. 3.7]
where a similar analysis is carried out for the event
E_{3}\cap(E_{2}^{\prime})^{c}\cap(E_{1}^{\prime})^{c}\cap E_{0}^{c}
. Using these steps or a standard information-theoretic analysis of binning it can be verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{\mathtt{T}_{1}\}+\mathbb{E}\{\mathtt{T}_{2}\}\leq\exp\left\{-n\left(\log q-H(p_{W})-5\eta-\frac{\beta\log q}{n}\right)\right\}+\exp\left\{-5n\eta\right\}.

Upper bound on
\norm{\ketbra{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}} -\ketbra{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}}_{1}
: While our analysis of this term is, in principle, similar to the corresponding term in proof of Thm.
2
, the fact that
\innerproduct{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}
is not necessarily
1
requires a refined analysis. We provide the same below. We adopt the following notational simplifications in the analysis of
\norm{\ketbra{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}} -\ketbra{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}}_{1}
.

Henceforth, we let
\varphi_{P}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\outerproduct{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}
and
\varphi_{\rho_{K}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\outerproduct{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}
. Our approach is to relate
\norm{\varphi_{P}-\varphi_{\rho_{K}}}_{1}
to
\norm{\sigma_{K}-\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}_{1}
and recognize the latter to be another instance of the quantum soft covering lemma (Lemma
9
). However,
\varphi_{\rho_{K}},\varphi_{P}
are purifications of
\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}
and
K^{-1}\sum_{k}T_{k}\otimes\outerproduct{k}{k}
respectively. How do we transfer an upper bound the trace distance between the states to an upper bound on the trace distance between their purifications. We do this through the relationship between trace distance and fidelity and the use of a few operator inequalities.

Let
a=1-\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{P}}
,
b=4\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{P}}-4
and
c=4-\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}\innerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}{\phi_{P}}
. Since
\innerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}=1
, we have
b+c=4\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{P}}\innerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}-\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}\innerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}{\phi_{P}}\geq 0
from the Cauchy-Bunyakowski-Schwartz inequality. In conjunction with Propositions
1
and
3
, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}=\norm{ \varphi_{\rho_{K}} - \varphi_{P}}_{1}\leq\sqrt{a^{2}+b+c}\leq a+\sqrt{b+c}

We begin analyzing
c
. Recalling
M,K
, we have

\displaystyle c\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!4-\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}\innerproduct{\phi_{\rho_{K}}}{\phi_{P}}

(159)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!4\left(1-\left[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\tr(\sqrt{T_{k}}\sqrt{\omega} )\right]^{2}\right)

(160)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!8\left(1-\left[\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k}\tr(\sqrt{T_{k}}\sqrt{\omega} )\right]\right)=\frac{8}{K}\sum_{k}\zeta_{k}

(161)

where (i) (
160
) follows from substitution, (ii) the inequality in (
161
) follows from Proposition
2
, and the equality in (
161
) follows from defining
\zeta_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.28473pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-3.28473pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}1-\tr(\sqrt{T_{k}}\sqrt{\omega})
. We have

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!=\!\!

\displaystyle\!1-\tr(\sqrt{T_{k}}\sqrt{\omega})=\tr(\rho)-\tr(\sqrt{T_{k}}\sqrt{\omega})

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\tr\left\{\sqrt{\omega}\left[\sqrt{\omega}-\sqrt{T_{k}}\right]\right\}\leq\norm{ \sqrt{\omega}\left[\sqrt{\omega}-\sqrt{T_{k}}\right]}_{1}

(163)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\norm{ \sqrt{\omega}}_{2}\norm{\sqrt{\omega}-\sqrt{T_{k}}}_{2}=\norm{\sqrt{\omega}-\sqrt{T_{k}}}_{2}

(164)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\norm{\sqrt{\left| \omega- T_{k} \right|}}_{2}=\sqrt{\norm{\left| \omega- T_{k} \right|}_{1}}

(165)

where (
162
) follows from
\tr(\rho)=1
, (
163
) follows from linearity of trace and the fact that
\tr(A)\leq\norm{A}_{1}
for any operator
A
, the first inequality in (
164
) follows from
[
36
, Corollary IV.2.6]
- a form of Hölder’s inequality, the equality in (
164
) follows from
\norm{\sqrt{\omega}}_{2}=\sqrt{\tr(\rho)}=1
, the first inequality in (
164
) follows from
[
36
, Theorem X.1.3]
and the equality in (
164
) follows from the definition of
|\cdot|
for an operator and the definition of
\norm{\cdot}_{1}
. Substituting this upper bound on
\zeta_{k}
in (
161
), we have

\displaystyle c\leq\frac{8}{K}\sum_{k}\sqrt{\norm{\left| \omega- T_{k} \right|}_{1}}.

(166)

Having completed our analysis of
c
, we now turn to
a,b
. Recall
a=1-\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{P}}
,
b=4\innerproduct{\phi_{P}}{\phi_{P}}-4
where
\ket{\phi_{P}}
was defined. Since
\ket{\phi_{P}}
is a purification of
2^{-C_{1}-C_{2}}\sum_{k}T_{k}\otimes\outerproduct{k}{k}
, we have
a=1-2^{-C_{1}-C_{2}}\sum_{k}\tr(T_{k})
. If we study
\mathbb{E}\{a\}
, we note that we are computing the
\mathbb{E}\{\tr(T_{k})\}
. Since each entry of the
X-
table is picked IID
p_{X}
and each entry of the
Y-
table is picked
p_{Y}
, it is a straightforward calculation to verify that
\mathbb{E}\{\tr(T_{k})\}=1
. This implies
\mathbb{E}\{a\}=\mathbb{E}\{b\}=0
. We are thus ready to evaluate the average value of
T_{2}
. Applying Jensen’s inequality to the concave
\sqrt{-}
function, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{T_{2}\}\leq\mathbb{E}\{a\}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\{b\}+\frac{8}{K}\sum_{k}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}{\norm{\left| \omega- T_{k} \right|}_{1}}}}

(167)

\mathbb{E}\{a\}=\mathbb{E}\{b\}=1-\mathbb{E}\{\tr(T_{k})\}
which can be easily verified to be
0
. We have thus reduced
\norm{\ketbra{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}}{\varphi_{\sigma_{K}}} -\ketbra{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}{\varphi_{\rho_{K}^{\otimes n}}}}_{1}
to an instance of the quantum soft covering lemma in Lemma
9
.
